## Consequential corrections

- 149_scan130, Right marginal note, opening: `C'est visiblement \uline{faux}` / `C'est \uncertain{sûrement} « \uncertain{en général} » \emph{faux}` → `C'est visiblement \uline{faux}`. The word after C'est begins v with a dot over the following i; two tall stems belong to b and l, followed by a compressed -ement ending. The separate faux has a large looped f and terminal crossed x and an underline.
- 149_scan130, Right marginal note, question about minimal model: `Mais y a-t-il un modèle « minimal » $X\supset U$` / `\textbf{Mais} \uncertain{est-il vrai} \uncertain{qu'on} \ill{} $X\supset U$` → `Mais y a-t-il un modèle &#96;&#96;minimal'' $X\supset U$`. After Mais a descending y and the short a-t-il sequence precede un. modèle has the same looped d and high l seen above. The following word has the low repeated minims of minimal and a high terminal l, bounded by pairs of quote ticks. X, the open superset sign and U stand on the next marginal baseline.
- 149_scan130, Right marginal note, end of question: `et pour un tel modèle \unsure{a-t-on} l'injectivité ???` / `\uncertain{tel que} \ill{} \uncertain{localement} \emph{injectif}…` → `et pour un tel modèle \unsure{a-t-on} l'injectivité ???`. The note contains a separate et with a crossed t, then pour with a p descender, un and tel modèle. The short following group is compressed at the edge and a-t-on remains doubtful. In the final word the j descends, the -tivité ending continues beyond the f-shaped middle stroke, and terminal question hooks/dots replace an ellipsis.
- 149_scan130, L12, parameter-space symbol: `$\unsure{\mathfrak X_{L/K}|\mathcal Z_{L/K}}$` / `$\mathfrak{X}_{L/K}$` → `$\mathfrak X_{L/K}$`. The main glyph has two upper arms meeting two lower curved arms at a central crossing, with a short transverse bar. It is X-shaped, with no Z-like top-to-bottom diagonal. The subscript is L / K. The whole short formula was read on the crop; this resolves C's alternative.
- 149_scan130, L13, first cancelled predicate: `\deleted{est injectif}` / `\struck{est égal}` → `\deleted{est injectif}`. Below the replacement the cancelled word includes a long j descender, then e/c, a tall crossed t, a dotted i and tall final f. This is the longer injectif, not égal; the single cancellation stroke crosses est and the whole predicate.
- 149_scan130, L18, stacked cancelled and replacement predicates: `\deleted{est injective} \deleted{\unsure{surjective}} \inserted{a une image ouverte}` / `\struck{soit \uncertain{à image} \ill{} \uncertain{ouverte}}` → `\deleted{est injective} \deleted{\inserted{\unsure{surjective}}} \inserted{a une image ouverte}`. The lowest pale baseline has est and a long j descender in injective crossed by a horizontal cancellation. Above it surjective is a separate interlinear correction, also crossed; overlapping strokes prevent full certainty in that word. Between/alongside these layers a une image ouverte is not crossed. The draft records the cancelled insertion state of surjective explicitly.
- 149_scan130, L18–19, later dark-ink conclusion: `\inserted{--- sans doute est-elle $=$ surjective.}` / `\add{\uncertain{soit}} surjective.` → `\inserted{--- sans doute est-elle $=$ surjective.}`. Above the cancelled pencil sentence the darker ink reads sans (initial two-lobed s), doute (looped d and crossed t), est-elle (two high l stems) and two horizontal equals strokes. The continuation surjective. is dark and live on the following baseline, also visible in crop P17. The black horizontal line below the first part cancels the older pencil sentence, not the new dark words.
- 149_scan132, L10 after justement: `justement, à sous-groupes` / `justement, à \uncertain{conjugaison} — et \uncertain{à} \uncertain{sous-groupes}` → `justement, à sous-groupes`. After the comma there is one accented à followed immediately by the short sous- group at the right end of this line. No intervening conjugaison, second dash, et, or second à is present.
- 149_scan132, L12 corrected relation after V: `V\deleted{\unsure{\to}}\inserted{\in}\mathfrak X_{L/K}` / `V \in \mathfrak{X}_{L/K} \struck{\ill{}}` → `V\deleted{\unsure{\to}}\inserted{\in}\mathfrak X_{L/K}`. - 149_scan132, L16 superscript on D_J: `D_J^{\unsure{\circ}}` / `D_J^{*}` → `D_J^*`. The raised mark has several crossing dark strokes and projecting tips around a filled centre; it is not a hollow circular superscript. The whole display was inspected with this mark. C’s doubt is resolved to an asterisk.
- 149_scan132, L16 brace label and right-hand closed intersection: `\overbrace{\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j}^{\Delta_J}-\bigcup_{i\in I-J}\Delta_J\cap D_i` / `\overbrace{\bigcap_{j \in J} D_j}^{D_J} - \bigcup_{i \in I \setminus J} D_J \cap D_i` → `\overbrace{\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j}^{\Delta_J}-\bigcup_{i\in I\setminus J}\Delta_J\cap D_i`. Both occurrences are closed triangular signs with a diagonal left side, diagonal right side and horizontal base, followed by J. The D_j under the brace instead has an upright stem and rounded bowl. Thus both closed-intersection labels are Delta_J, not D_J.
- 149_scan132, L17 cardinality variable: `d=\operatorname{card}J` / `\alpha = \operatorname{card} J` → `d=\operatorname{card}J`. The variable has a small rounded bowl and a separate high ascender on its right, the same d form as the subsequent codimension variable; an alpha has no such tall d stem here.
- 149_scan132, L18 de codim d: `de codim $d$` / `de codim $\alpha$` → `de codim $d$`. The variable after codim again has a round low bowl with a tall upright ascender, matching the d in d=card J; the ascender is the distinguishing stroke.
- 149_scan132, Margin underlined sous-schéma: `\uline{sous-schéma}` / `\uncertain{l'ensemble}` → `\uline{sous-schéma}`. The underlined group visibly begins sous, has a separated short hyphen, then the tall sch sequence and final ma. The underline runs under this word, which cannot be reduced to l’ensemble.
- 149_scan132, Margin définir son support / sous-ouvert: `définir son \uline{\unsure{support|sous-ouvert}}` / `\uncertain{décrire}` → `définir son \uline{\unsure{support|sous-ouvert}}`. The underlined word has tall descending central strokes and a final t-like stroke, but the compressed joining of the middle letters does not securely distinguish support from sous-ouvert. H omits son and the whole underlined group; C’s unresolved alternatives are retained.
- 149_scan132, Margin final set relation: `I(x)\unsure{=|\supseteq}J` / `I(x) \supset J` → `I(x)=J`. Between the closing parenthesis and J there are two separate heavy horizontal bars of equal orientation. There is no right-facing curved or angular enclosure at their right ends. - 149_scan133, Top interlinear parenthesis, whole formula: `\inserted{(i.e. $\unsure{\underline{D}_J|\bar D_J}=\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j$ \unsure{soit} $\ne\varnothing$)}` / `\add{(i.e.\ $D_J = \bigcap_{j \in J} D_j$ soit $\neq \emptyset$)},` → `\inserted{(i.e. $\bar D_J=\bigcap_{j\in J}D_j$ soit $\ne\phi$)}`. - 149_scan133, L2, first replaced noun and adjective: `\deleted{\unsure{solide}} \inserted{maquette} simpliciale` / `\struck{schéma} \add{\uncertain{ensemble}} simplicial` → `\deleted{\unsure{solide}} \inserted{maquette} simpliciale`.
